<ROLE>
Your primary role is to assist users by executing commands, modifying code, and solving technical problems effectively. You should be thorough, methodical, and prioritize quality over speed.
* If the user asks a question, like "why is X happening", don't try to fix the problem. Just give an answer to the question.
</ROLE>

<EFFICIENCY>
* Each action you take is somewhat expensive. Wherever possible, combine multiple actions into a single action, e.g. combine multiple bash commands into one, using sed and grep to edit/view multiple files at once.
* When exploring the codebase, use efficient tools like find, grep, and git commands with appropriate filters to minimize unnecessary operations.
</EFFICIENCY>

<FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>
* When a user provides a file path, do NOT assume it's relative to the current working directory. First explore the file system to locate the file before working on it.
* If asked to edit a file, edit the file directly, rather than creating a new file with a different filename.
* For global search-and-replace operations, consider using `sed` instead of opening file editors multiple times.
* NEVER create multiple versions of the same file with different suffixes (e.g., file_test.py, file_fix.py, file_simple.py). Instead:
  - Always modify the original file directly when making changes
  - If you need to create a temporary file for testing, delete it once you've confirmed your solution works
  - If you decide a file you created is no longer useful, delete it instead of creating a new version
* Do NOT include documentation files explaining your changes in version control unless the user explicitly requests it
* When reproducing bugs or implementing fixes, use a single file rather than creating multiple files with different versions
</FILE_SYSTEM_GUIDELINES>

<CODE_QUALITY>
* Write clean, efficient code with minimal comments. Avoid redundancy in comments: Do not repeat information that can be easily inferred from the code itself.
* When implementing solutions, focus on making the minimal changes needed to solve the problem.
* Before implementing any changes, first thoroughly understand the codebase through exploration.
* If you are adding a lot of code to a function or file, consider splitting the function or file into smaller pieces when appropriate.
* Place all imports at the top of the file unless explicitly requested otherwise or if placing imports at the top would cause issues (e.g., circular imports, conditional imports, or imports that need to be delayed for specific reasons).
</CODE_QUALITY>

<VERSION_CONTROL>
* If there are existing git user credentials already configured, use them and add Co-authored-by: openhands <openhands@all-hands.dev> to any commits messages you make. if a git config doesn't exist use "openhands" as the user.name and "openhands@all-hands.dev" as the user.email by default, unless explicitly instructed otherwise.
* Exercise caution with git operations. Do NOT make potentially dangerous changes (e.g., pushing to main, deleting repositories) unless explicitly asked to do so.
* When committing changes, use `git status` to see all modified files, and stage all files necessary for the commit. Use `git commit -a` whenever possible.
* Do NOT commit files that typically shouldn't go into version control (e.g., node_modules/, .env files, build directories, cache files, large binaries) unless explicitly instructed by the user.
* If unsure about committing certain files, check for the presence of .gitignore files or ask the user for clarification.
</VERSION_CONTROL>

<PULL_REQUESTS>
* **Important**: Do not push to the remote branch and/or start a pull request unless explicitly asked to do so.

</PULL_REQUESTS>

</DOCUMENTATION>

<PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>
* When terminating processes:
  - Do NOT use general keywords with commands like `pkill -f server` or `pkill -f python` as this might accidentally kill other important servers or processes
  - Always use specific keywords that uniquely identify the target process
  - Prefer using `ps aux` to find the exact process ID (PID) first, then kill that specific PID
  - When possible, use more targeted approaches like finding the PID from a pidfile or using application-specific shutdown commands
</PROCESS_MANAGEMENT>

     1	# KerasCV
     2	
     3	[![](https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/workflows/Tests/badge.svg?branch=master)](https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/actions?query=workflow%3ATests+branch%3Amaster)
     4	![Downloads](https://img.shields.io/pypi/dm/keras-cv.svg)
     5	![Python](https://img.shields.io/badge/python-v3.7.0+-success.svg)
     6	![Tensorflow](https://img.shields.io/badge/tensorflow-v2.9.0+-success.svg)
     7	[![Contributions Welcome](https://img.shields.io/badge/contributions-welcome-brightgreen.svg?style=flat)](https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/issues)
     8	
     9	KerasCV is a library of modular computer vision oriented Keras components.
    10	These components include models, layers, metrics, losses, callbacks, and utility
    11	functions.
    12	
    13	KerasCV's primary goal is to provide a coherent, elegant, and pleasant API to train state of the art computer vision models.
    14	Users should be able to train state of the art models using only `Keras`, `KerasCV`, and TensorFlow core (i.e. `tf.data`) components.
    15	
    16	KerasCV can be understood as a horizontal extension of the Keras API: the components are new first-party
    17	Keras objects (layers, metrics, etc.) that are too specialized to be added to core Keras. They receive the same level of polish and backwards compatibility guarantees as the core Keras API, and they are maintained by the Keras team.
    18	
    19	Our APIs assist in common computer vision tasks such as data-augmentation, classification, object detection, image generation, and more.
    20	Applied computer vision engineers can leverage KerasCV to quickly assemble production-grade, state-of-the-art training and inference pipelines for all of these common tasks.
    21	
    22	In addition to API consistency, KerasCV components aim to be mixed-precision compatible, QAT compatible, XLA compilable, and TPU compatible.
    23	We also aim to provide generic model optimization tools for deployment on devices such as onboard GPUs, mobile, and edge chips.
    24	
    25	
    26	To learn more about the future project direction, please check the [roadmap](.github/ROADMAP.md).
    27	
    28	## Quick Links
    29	- [Contributing Guide](.github/CONTRIBUTING.md)
    30	- [Call for Contributions](https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/issues?q=is%3Aopen+is%3Aissue+label%3Acontribution-welcome)
    31	- [Roadmap](.github/ROADMAP.md)
    32	- [API Design Guidelines](.github/API_DESIGN.md)
    33	
    34	## Installation
    35	
    36	To install the latest official release:
    37	
    38	```
    39	pip install keras-cv tensorflow --upgrade
    40	```
    41	
    42	To install the latest unreleased changes to the library, we recommend using
    43	pip to install directly from the master branch on github:
    44	
    45	```
    46	pip install git+https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv.git tensorflow --upgrade
    47	```
    48	
    49	## Quickstart
    50	
    51	```python
    52	import keras_cv
    53	import tensorflow as tf
    54	from tensorflow import keras
    55	import tensorflow_datasets as tfds
    56	
    57	# Create a preprocessing pipeline
    58	augmenter = keras.Sequential(
    59	    layers=[
    60	        keras_cv.layers.RandomFlip(),
    61	        keras_cv.layers.RandAugment(value_range=(0, 255)),
    62	        keras_cv.layers.CutMix(),
    63	        keras_cv.layers.MixUp()
    64	    ]
    65	)
    66	
    67	def preprocess_data(images, labels, augment=False):
    68	    labels = tf.one_hot(labels, 3)
    69	    inputs = {"images": images, "labels": labels}
    70	    outputs = augmenter(inputs) if augment else inputs
    71	    return outputs['images'], outputs['labels']
    72	
    73	# Augment a `tf.data.Dataset`
    74	train_dataset, test_dataset = tfds.load(
    75	    'rock_paper_scissors',
    76	    as_supervised=True,
    77	    split=['train', 'test'],
    78	)
    79	train_dataset = train_dataset.batch(16).map(
    80	    lambda x, y: preprocess_data(x, y, augment=True),
    81	        num_parallel_calls=tf.data.AUTOTUNE).prefetch(
    82	            tf.data.AUTOTUNE)
    83	test_dataset = test_dataset.batch(16).map(
    84	    preprocess_data, num_parallel_calls=tf.data.AUTOTUNE).prefetch(
    85	        tf.data.AUTOTUNE)
    86	
    87	# Create a model
    88	densenet = keras_cv.models.DenseNet121(
    89	    include_rescaling=True,
    90	    include_top=True,
    91	    num_classes=3
    92	)
    93	densenet.compile(
    94	    loss='categorical_crossentropy',
    95	    optimizer='adam',
    96	    metrics=['accuracy']
    97	)
    98	
    99	# Train your model
   100	densenet.fit(train_dataset, validation_data=test_dataset)
   101	```
   102	
   103	## Contributors
   104	If you'd like to contribute, please see our [contributing guide](.github/CONTRIBUTING.md).
   105	
   106	To find an issue to tackle, please check our [call for contributions](.github/CALL_FOR_CONTRIBUTIONS.md).
   107	
   108	We would like to leverage/outsource the Keras community not only for bug reporting,
   109	but also for active development for feature delivery. To achieve this, here is the predefined
   110	process for how to contribute to this repository:
   111	
   112	1) Contributors are always welcome to help us fix an issue, add tests, better documentation.  
   113	2) If contributors would like to create a backbone, we usually require a pre-trained weight set
   114	with the model for one dataset as the first PR, and a training script as a follow-up. The training script will preferrably help us reproduce the results claimed from paper. The backbone should be generic but the training script can contain paper specific parameters such as learning rate schedules and weight decays. The training script will be used to produce leaderboard results.  
   115	Exceptions apply to large transformer-based models which are difficult to train. If this is the case,
   116	contributors should let us know so the team can help in training the model or providing GCP resources.
   117	3) If contributors would like to create a meta arch, please try to be aligned with our roadmap and create a PR for design review to make sure the meta arch is modular.
   118	4) If contributors would like to create a new input formatting which is not in our roadmap for the next 6 months, e.g., keypoint, please create an issue and ask for a sponsor.
   119	5) If contributors would like to support a new task which is not in our roadmap for the next 6 months, e.g., 3D reconstruction, please create an issue and ask for a sponsor.
   120	
   121	Thank you to all of our wonderful contributors!
   122	
   123	<a href="https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/graphs/contributors">
   124	  <img src="https://contrib.rocks/image?repo=keras-team/keras-cv" />
   125	</a>
   126	
   127	## Pretrained Weights
   128	Many models in KerasCV come with pre-trained weights.
   129	With the exception of StableDiffusion and the standard Vision Transformer, all of these weights are trained using Keras and
   130	KerasCV components and training scripts in this repository.
   131	While some models are not trained with the same parameters or preprocessing pipeline
   132	as defined in their original publications, the KerasCV team ensures strong numerical performance.
   133	Performance metrics for the provided pre-trained weights can be found
   134	in the training history for each documented task.
   135	An example of this can be found in the ImageNet classification training
   136	[history for backbone models](examples/training/classification/imagenet/training_history.json).
   137	All results are reproducible using the training scripts in this repository.
   138	
   139	Historically, many models have been trained on image datasets rescaled via manually
   140	crafted normalization schemes.  
   141	The most common variant of manually crafted normalization scheme is subtraction of the
   142	imagenet mean pixel followed by standard deviation normalization based on the imagenet
   143	pixel standard deviation.
   144	This scheme is an artifact of the days of manual feature engineering, but is no longer
   145	required to score state of the art scores using modern deep learning architectures.
   146	Due to this, KerasCV is standardized to operate on images that have been rescaled using
   147	a simple `1/255` rescaling layer.
   148	This can be seen in all KerasCV training pipelines and code examples.
   149	
   150	## Custom Ops
   151	Note that in some of the 3D Object Detection layers, custom TF ops are used. The
   152	binaries for these ops are not shipped in our PyPi package in order to keep our
   153	wheels pure-Python.
   154	
   155	If you'd like to use these custom ops, you can install from source using the
   156	instructions below.
   157	
   158	### Installing KerasCV with Custom Ops from Source
   159	
   160	Installing custom ops from source requires the [Bazel](https://bazel.build/) build
   161	system (version >= 5.4.0).  Steps to install Bazel can be [found here](https://github.com/keras-team/keras/blob/v2.11.0/.devcontainer/Dockerfile#L21-L23).
   162	
   163	```
   164	git clone https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv.git
   165	cd keras-cv
   166	
   167	python3 build_deps/configure.py
   168	
   169	bazel build build_pip_pkg
   170	export BUILD_WITH_CUSTOM_OPS=true
   171	bazel-bin/build_pip_pkg wheels
   172	
   173	pip install wheels/keras_cv-*.whl
   174	```
   175	
   176	Note that GitHub actions exist to release KerasCV with custom ops, but are
   177	currently disabled. You can use these [actions](https://github.com/keras-team/keras-cv/blob/master/.github/workflows/release.yml)
   178	in your own fork to create wheels for Linux (manylinux2014), MacOS (both x86 and ARM),
   179	and Windows.
   180

   185	or conditions of any kind.
   186	The following underlying models are provided by third parties, and are subject to separate

    14	"""
    15	random_shear_demo.py shows how to use the RandomShear preprocessing layer
    16	for object detection.
    17	"""
    18	import demo_utils
    19	import tensorflow as tf
    20	
    21	from keras_cv.layers import preprocessing
    22	
    23	
    24	def main():
    25	    dataset = demo_utils.load_voc_dataset(bounding_box_format="xyxy")
    26	    random_shear = preprocessing.RandomShear(
    27	        x_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
    28	        y_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
    29	        bounding_box_format="xyxy",
    30	    )
    31	    dataset = dataset.map(random_shear, num_parallel_calls=tf.data.AUTOTUNE)
    32	    demo_utils.visualize_data(dataset, bounding_box_format="xyxy")
    33	
    34	
    35	if __name__ == "__main__":
    36	    main()

     1	# Copyright 2022 The KerasCV Authors
     2	#
     3	# Licensed under the Apache License, Version 2.0 (the "License");
     4	# you may not use this file except in compliance with the License.
     5	# You may obtain a copy of the License at
     6	#
     7	#     https://www.apache.org/licenses/LICENSE-2.0
     8	#
     9	# Unless required by applicable law or agreed to in writing, software
    10	# distributed under the License is distributed on an "AS IS" BASIS,
    11	# WITHOUT WARRANTIES OR CONDITIONS OF ANY KIND, either express or implied.
    12	# See the License for the specific language governing permissions and
    13	# limitations under the License.
    14	"""Utility functions for preprocessing demos."""
    15	import matplotlib.pyplot as plt
    16	import numpy as np
    17	import tensorflow as tf
    18	import tensorflow_datasets as tfds
    19	
    20	from keras_cv import bounding_box
    21	
    22	
    23	def preprocess_voc(inputs, format):
    24	    image = inputs["image"]
    25	    image = tf.cast(image, tf.float32)
    26	    boxes = inputs["objects"]["bbox"]
    27	    boxes = bounding_box.convert_format(
    28	        boxes,
    29	        images=image,
    30	        source="rel_yxyx",
    31	        target=format,
    32	    )
    33	    classes = tf.cast(inputs["objects"]["label"], tf.float32)
    34	    bounding_boxes = {"classes": classes, "boxes": boxes}
    35	    return {"images": image, "bounding_boxes": bounding_boxes}
    36	
    37	
    38	def load_voc_dataset(
    39	    bounding_box_format,
    40	    name="voc/2007",
    41	    batch_size=9,
    42	):
    43	    dataset = tfds.load(name, split=tfds.Split.TRAIN, shuffle_files=True)
    44	    dataset = dataset.map(
    45	        lambda x: preprocess_voc(x, format=bounding_box_format),
    46	        num_parallel_calls=tf.data.AUTOTUNE,
    47	    )
    48	    dataset = dataset.apply(
    49	        tf.data.experimental.dense_to_ragged_batch(batch_size)
    50	    )
    51	    return dataset
    52	
    53	
    54	def visualize_data(data, bounding_box_format):
    55	    data = next(iter(data))
    56	    images = data["images"]
    57	    bounding_boxes = data["bounding_boxes"]
    58	    output_images = visualize_bounding_boxes(
    59	        images, bounding_boxes, bounding_box_format
    60	    ).numpy()
    61	    gallery_show(output_images)
    62	
    63	
    64	def visualize_bounding_boxes(image, bounding_boxes, bounding_box_format):
    65	    color = np.array([[255.0, 0.0, 0.0]])
    66	    bounding_boxes = bounding_box.to_dense(bounding_boxes)
    67	    bounding_boxes = bounding_box.convert_format(
    68	        bounding_boxes,
    69	        source=bounding_box_format,
    70	        target="yxyx",
    71	        images=image,
    72	    )
    73	    if isinstance(image, tf.RaggedTensor):
    74	        image = image.to_tensor(0)
    75	    bounding_boxes = bounding_box.convert_format(
    76	        bounding_boxes,
    77	        source="yxyx",
    78	        target="rel_yxyx",
    79	        images=image,
    80	    )
    81	    bounding_boxes = bounding_boxes["boxes"]
    82	    return tf.image.draw_bounding_boxes(image, bounding_boxes, color, name=None)
    83	
    84	
    85	def gallery_show(images):
    86	    images = images.astype(int)
    87	    for i in range(9):
    88	        image = images[i]
    89	        plt.subplot(3, 3, i + 1)
    90	        plt.imshow(image.astype("uint8"))
    91	        plt.axis("off")
    92	    plt.show()

    14	"""Converter functions for working with bounding box formats."""

    16	from typing import List

    24	# Internal exception to propagate the fact images was not passed to a converter that

    26	class RequiresImagesException(Exception):
    27	    pass

    30	ALL_AXES = [1, 1, 1, 1]
    31	
    32	
    33	def _encode_box_to_deltas(
    34	    anchors: tf.Tensor,
    35	    boxes: tf.Tensor,
    36	    anchor_format: str,
    37	    box_format: str,
    38	    variance: Optional[Union[List[float], tf.Tensor]] = None,
    39	):
    40	    """Converts bounding_boxes from `center_yxhw` to delta format."""
    41	    if variance is not None:
    42	        if tf.is_tensor(variance):
    43	            var_len = variance.get_shape().as_list()[-1]
    44	        else:
    45	            var_len = len(variance)
    46	        if var_len != 4:
    47	            raise ValueError(f"`variance` must be length 4, got {variance}")
    48	    encoded_anchors = convert_format(
    49	        anchors,
    50	        source=anchor_format,
    51	        target="center_yxhw",
    52	    )
    53	    boxes = convert_format(
    54	        boxes,
    55	        source=box_format,
    56	        target="center_yxhw",
    57	    )
    58	    anchor_dimensions = tf.maximum(
    59	        encoded_anchors[..., 2:], keras.backend.epsilon()
    60	    )
    61	    box_dimensions = tf.maximum(boxes[..., 2:], keras.backend.epsilon())
    62	    # anchors be unbatched, boxes can either be batched or unbatched.
    63	    boxes_delta = tf.concat(
    64	        [
    65	            (boxes[..., :2] - encoded_anchors[..., :2]) / anchor_dimensions,
    66	            tf.math.log(box_dimensions / anchor_dimensions),
    67	        ],
    68	        axis=-1,
    69	    )
    70	    if variance is not None:
    71	        boxes_delta /= variance
    72	    return boxes_delta
    73	
    74	
    75	def _decode_deltas_to_boxes(
    76	    anchors: tf.Tensor,
    77	    boxes_delta: tf.Tensor,
    78	    anchor_format: str,
    79	    box_format: str,
    80	    variance: Optional[Union[List[float], tf.Tensor]] = None,
    81	):
    82	    """Converts bounding_boxes from delta format to `center_yxhw`."""
    83	    if variance is not None:
    84	        if tf.is_tensor(variance):
    85	            var_len = variance.get_shape().as_list()[-1]
    86	        else:
    87	            var_len = len(variance)
    88	        if var_len != 4:
    89	            raise ValueError(f"`variance` must be length 4, got {variance}")
    90	    tf.nest.assert_same_structure(anchors, boxes_delta)
    91	
    92	    def decode_single_level(anchor, box_delta):
    93	        encoded_anchor = convert_format(
    94	            anchor,
    95	            source=anchor_format,
    96	            target="center_yxhw",
    97	        )
    98	        if variance is not None:
    99	            box_delta = box_delta * variance
   100	        # anchors be unbatched, boxes can either be batched or unbatched.
   101	        box = tf.concat(
   102	            [
   103	                box_delta[..., :2] * encoded_anchor[..., 2:]
   104	                + encoded_anchor[..., :2],
   105	                tf.math.exp(box_delta[..., 2:]) * encoded_anchor[..., 2:],
   106	            ],
   107	            axis=-1,
   108	        )
   109	        box = convert_format(box, source="center_yxhw", target=box_format)
   110	        return box
   111	
   112	    if isinstance(anchors, dict) and isinstance(boxes_delta, dict):
   113	        boxes = {}
   114	        for lvl, anchor in anchors.items():
   115	            boxes[lvl] = decode_single_level(anchor, boxes_delta[lvl])
   116	        return boxes
   117	    else:
   118	        return decode_single_level(anchors, boxes_delta)
   119	
   120	
   121	def _center_yxhw_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   122	    y, x, height, width = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   123	    return tf.concat(
   124	        [x - width / 2.0, y - height / 2.0, x + width / 2.0, y + height / 2.0],
   125	        axis=-1,
   126	    )
   127	
   128	
   129	def _center_xywh_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   130	    x, y, width, height = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   131	    return tf.concat(
   132	        [x - width / 2.0, y - height / 2.0, x + width / 2.0, y + height / 2.0],
   133	        axis=-1,
   134	    )
   135	
   136	
   137	def _xywh_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   138	    x, y, width, height = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   139	    return tf.concat([x, y, x + width, y + height], axis=-1)
   140	
   141	
   142	def _xyxy_to_center_yxhw(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   143	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   144	    return tf.concat(
   145	        [
   146	            (top + bottom) / 2.0,
   147	            (left + right) / 2.0,
   148	            bottom - top,
   149	            right - left,
   150	        ],
   151	        axis=-1,
   152	    )
   153	
   154	
   155	def _rel_xywh_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   156	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   157	    x, y, width, height = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   158	    return tf.concat(
   159	        [
   160	            image_width * x,
   161	            image_height * y,
   162	            image_width * (x + width),
   163	            image_height * (y + height),
   164	        ],
   165	        axis=-1,
   166	    )
   167	
   168	
   169	def _xyxy_no_op(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   170	    return boxes
   171	
   172	
   173	def _xyxy_to_xywh(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   174	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   175	    return tf.concat(
   176	        [left, top, right - left, bottom - top],
   177	        axis=-1,
   178	    )
   179	
   180	
   181	def _xyxy_to_rel_xywh(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   182	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   183	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   184	    left, right = (
   185	        left / image_width,
   186	        right / image_width,
   187	    )
   188	    top, bottom = top / image_height, bottom / image_height
   189	    return tf.concat(
   190	        [left, top, right - left, bottom - top],
   191	        axis=-1,
   192	    )
   193	
   194	
   195	def _xyxy_to_center_xywh(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   196	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   197	    return tf.concat(
   198	        [
   199	            (left + right) / 2.0,
   200	            (top + bottom) / 2.0,
   201	            right - left,
   202	            bottom - top,
   203	        ],
   204	        axis=-1,
   205	    )
   206	
   207	
   208	def _rel_xyxy_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   209	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   210	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(
   211	        boxes,
   212	        ALL_AXES,
   213	        axis=-1,
   214	    )
   215	    left, right = left * image_width, right * image_width
   216	    top, bottom = top * image_height, bottom * image_height
   217	    return tf.concat(
   218	        [left, top, right, bottom],
   219	        axis=-1,
   220	    )
   221	
   222	
   223	def _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   224	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   225	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(
   226	        boxes,
   227	        ALL_AXES,
   228	        axis=-1,
   229	    )
   230	    left, right = left / image_width, right / image_width
   231	    top, bottom = top / image_height, bottom / image_height
   232	    return tf.concat(
   233	        [left, top, right, bottom],
   234	        axis=-1,
   235	    )
   236

   238	def _yxyx_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   239	    y1, x1, y2, x2 = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   240	    return tf.concat([x1, y1, x2, y2], axis=-1)
   241	
   242	
   243	def _rel_yxyx_to_xyxy(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   244	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   245	    top, left, bottom, right = tf.split(
   246	        boxes,
   247	        ALL_AXES,
   248	        axis=-1,
   249	    )
   250	    left, right = left * image_width, right * image_width
   251	    top, bottom = top * image_height, bottom * image_height
   252	    return tf.concat(
   253	        [left, top, right, bottom],
   254	        axis=-1,
   255	    )
   256	
   257	
   258	def _xyxy_to_yxyx(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   259	    x1, y1, x2, y2 = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   260	    return tf.concat([y1, x1, y2, x2], axis=-1)
   261	
   262	
   263	def _xyxy_to_rel_yxyx(boxes, images=None, image_shape=None):
   264	    image_height, image_width = _image_shape(images, image_shape, boxes)
   265	    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
   266	    left, right = left / image_width, right / image_width
   267	    top, bottom = top / image_height, bottom / image_height
   268	    return tf.concat(
   269	        [top, left, bottom, right],
   270	        axis=-1,
   271	    )

   274	TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS = {
   275	    "xywh": _xywh_to_xyxy,
   276	    "center_xywh": _center_xywh_to_xyxy,
   277	    "center_yxhw": _center_yxhw_to_xyxy,
   278	    "rel_xywh": _rel_xywh_to_xyxy,
   279	    "xyxy": _xyxy_no_op,
   280	    "rel_xyxy": _rel_xyxy_to_xyxy,
   281	    "yxyx": _yxyx_to_xyxy,
   282	    "rel_yxyx": _rel_yxyx_to_xyxy,
   283	}
   284	
   285	FROM_XYXY_CONVERTERS = {
   286	    "xywh": _xyxy_to_xywh,
   287	    "center_xywh": _xyxy_to_center_xywh,
   288	    "center_yxhw": _xyxy_to_center_yxhw,
   289	    "rel_xywh": _xyxy_to_rel_xywh,
   290	    "xyxy": _xyxy_no_op,
   291	    "rel_xyxy": _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy,
   292	    "yxyx": _xyxy_to_yxyx,
   293	    "rel_yxyx": _xyxy_to_rel_yxyx,
   294	}

   296	
   297	def convert_format(
   298	    boxes, source, target, images=None, image_shape=None, dtype="float32"
   299	):
   300	    f"""Converts bounding_boxes from one format to another.
   301	
   302	    Supported formats are:
   303	    - `"xyxy"`, also known as `corners` format.  In this format the first four axes
   304	        represent `[left, top, right, bottom]` in that order.
   305	    - `"rel_xyxy"`.  In this format, the axes are the same as `"xyxy"` but the x
   306	        coordinates are normalized using the image width, and the y axes the image
   307	        height.  All values in `rel_xyxy` are in the range `(0, 1)`.
   308	    - `"xywh"`.  In this format the first four axes represent
   309	        `[left, top, width, height]`.
   310	    - `"rel_xywh".  In this format the first four axes represent
   311	        [left, top, width, height], just like `"xywh"`.  Unlike `"xywh"`, the values
   312	        are in the range (0, 1) instead of absolute pixel values.
   313	    - `"center_xyWH"`.  In this format the first two coordinates represent the x and y
   314	        coordinates of the center of the bounding box, while the last two represent
   315	        the width and height of the bounding box.
   316	    - `"center_yxHW"`.  In this format the first two coordinates represent the y and x
   317	        coordinates of the center of the bounding box, while the last two represent
   318	        the height and width of the bounding box.
   319	    - `"yxyx"`.  In this format the first four axes represent [top, left, bottom, right]
   320	        in that order.
   321	    - `"rel_yxyx"`.  In this format, the axes are the same as `"yxyx"` but the x
   322	        coordinates are normalized using the image width, and the y axes the image
   323	        height.  All values in `rel_yxyx` are in the range (0, 1).
   324	    Formats are case insensitive.  It is recommended that you capitalize width and
   325	    height to maximize the visual difference between `"xyWH"` and `"xyxy"`.
   326	
   327	    Relative formats, abbreviated `rel`, make use of the shapes of the `images` passed.
   328	    In these formats, the coordinates, widths, and heights are all specified as
   329	    percentages of the host image.  `images` may be a ragged Tensor.  Note that using a
   330	    ragged Tensor for images may cause a substantial performance loss, as each image
   331	    will need to be processed separately due to the mismatching image shapes.
   332	
   333	    Usage:
   334	
   335	    ```python
   336	    boxes = load_coco_dataset()
   337	    boxes_in_xywh = keras_cv.bounding_box.convert_format(
   338	        boxes,
   339	        source='xyxy',
   340	        target='xyWH'
   341	    )
   342	    ```
   343	
   344	    Args:
   345	        boxes: tf.Tensor representing bounding boxes in the format specified in the
   346	            `source` parameter.  `boxes` can optionally have extra dimensions stacked on
   347	             the final axis to store metadata.  boxes should be a 3D Tensor, with the
   348	             shape `[batch_size, num_boxes, 4]`.  Alternatively, boxes can be a
   349	             dictionary with key 'boxes' containing a Tensor matching the aforementioned
   350	             spec.
   351	        source: One of {" ".join([f'"{f}"' for f in TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS.keys()])}.  Used
   352	            to specify the original format of the `boxes` parameter.
   353	        target: One of {" ".join([f'"{f}"' for f in TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS.keys()])}.  Used
   354	            to specify the destination format of the `boxes` parameter.
   355	        images: (Optional) a batch of images aligned with `boxes` on the first axis.
   356	            Should be at least 3 dimensions, with the first 3 dimensions representing:
   357	            `[batch_size, height, width]`.  Used in some converters to compute relative
   358	            pixel values of the bounding box dimensions.  Required when transforming
   359	            from a rel format to a non-rel format.
   360	        dtype: the data type to use when transforming the boxes.  Defaults to
   361	            `tf.float32`.
   362	    """
   363	    if isinstance(boxes, dict):
   364	        boxes["boxes"] = convert_format(
   365	            boxes["boxes"],
   366	            source=source,
   367	            target=target,
   368	            images=images,
   369	            image_shape=image_shape,
   370	            dtype=dtype,
   371	        )
   372	        return boxes
   373	
   374	    if boxes.shape[-1] != 4:
   375	        raise ValueError(
   376	            "Expected `boxes` to be a Tensor with a "
   377	            f"final dimension of `4`. Instead, got `boxes.shape={boxes.shape}`."
   378	        )
   379	    if images is not None and image_shape is not None:
   380	        raise ValueError(
   381	            "convert_format() expects either `images` or `image_shape`, "
   382	            f"but not both.  Received images={images} image_shape={image_shape}"
   383	        )
   384	
   385	    _validate_image_shape(image_shape)
   386	
   387	    source = source.lower()
   388	    target = target.lower()
   389	    if source not in TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS:
   390	        raise ValueError(
   391	            f"`convert_format()` received an unsupported format for the argument "
   392	            f"`source`.  `source` should be one of {TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS.keys()}. "
   393	            f"Got source={source}"
   394	        )
   395	    if target not in FROM_XYXY_CONVERTERS:
   396	        raise ValueError(
   397	            f"`convert_format()` received an unsupported format for the argument "
   398	            f"`target`.  `target` should be one of {FROM_XYXY_CONVERTERS.keys()}. "
   399	            f"Got target={target}"
   400	        )
   401	
   402	    boxes = tf.cast(boxes, dtype)
   403	    if source == target:
   404	        return boxes
   405	
   406	    # rel->rel conversions should not require images
   407	    if source.startswith("rel") and target.startswith("rel"):
   408	        source = source.replace("rel_", "", 1)
   409	        target = target.replace("rel_", "", 1)
   410	
   411	    boxes, images, squeeze = _format_inputs(boxes, images)
   412	    to_xyxy_fn = TO_XYXY_CONVERTERS[source]
   413	    from_xyxy_fn = FROM_XYXY_CONVERTERS[target]
   414	
   415	    try:
   416	        in_xyxy = to_xyxy_fn(boxes, images=images, image_shape=image_shape)
   417	        result = from_xyxy_fn(in_xyxy, images=images, image_shape=image_shape)
   418	    except RequiresImagesException:
   419	        raise ValueError(
   420	            "convert_format() must receive `images` or `image_shape` when transforming "
   421	            f"between relative and absolute formats."
   422	            f"convert_format() received source=`{format}`, target=`{format}, "
   423	            f"but images={images} and image_shape={image_shape}."
   424	        )
   425	
   426	    return _format_outputs(result, squeeze)
   427	
   428	
   429	def _format_inputs(boxes, images):
   430	    boxes_rank = len(boxes.shape)
   431	    if boxes_rank > 3:
   432	        raise ValueError(
   433	            "Expected len(boxes.shape)=2, or len(boxes.shape)=3, got "
   434	            f"len(boxes.shape)={boxes_rank}"
   435	        )
   436	    boxes_includes_batch = boxes_rank == 3
   437	    # Determine if images needs an expand_dims() call
   438	    if images is not None:
   439	        images_rank = len(images.shape)
   440	        if images_rank > 4:
   441	            raise ValueError(
   442	                "Expected len(images.shape)=2, or len(images.shape)=3, got "
   443	                f"len(images.shape)={images_rank}"
   444	            )
   445	        images_include_batch = images_rank == 4
   446	        if boxes_includes_batch != images_include_batch:
   447	            raise ValueError(
   448	                "convert_format() expects both boxes and images to be batched, or both "
   449	                f"boxes and images to be unbatched.  Received len(boxes.shape)={boxes_rank}, "
   450	                f"len(images.shape)={images_rank}.  Expected eithe<response clipped><NOTE>Due to the max output limit, only part of this file has been shown to you. </tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now let me understand the issue better. Looking at the problem description and the code, I need to understand what happens when `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` operates on ragged images. Let me search for the specific issue mentioned in the GitHub link:<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Looking at the issue description and the code, I can see that:

1. The issue is in `bounding_box.converts._xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` 
2. It breaks when operating on ragged images
3. There's a demo file that shows this breaking: `examples/layers/random_shear_demo.py`

Looking at the `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` function (lines 223-235), it calls `_image_shape` to get the image dimensions, and then performs division operations on the boxes coordinates with the image width and height.

The problem seems to be in the `_image_shape` function (lines 501-519). When dealing with RaggedTensors, it returns height and width as tensors with specific shapes, but the subsequent division operations in `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` might not handle these shapes properly.

Looking at lines 509-516 in `_image_shape`, when images is a RaggedTensor:
- height = tf.reshape(images.row_lengths(), (-1, 1)) - shape (batch_size, 1)
- width = tf.reshape(tf.reduce_max(images.row_lengths(axis=2), 1), (-1, 1)) - shape (batch_size, 1)
- If boxes is also a RaggedTensor, height and width get expanded dims

But in `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` (lines 223-235), the division operations happen:
- left, right = left / image_width, right / image_width
- top, bottom = top / image_height, bottom / image_height

The issue is likely that when we have ragged tensors, the shapes of the boxes coordinates and the image dimensions don't align properly for broadcasting in the division operations.

"""
Script to reproduce the issue with _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy when operating on ragged images.
"""

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf
from keras_cv import bounding_box

def test_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy_with_ragged_images():
    """Test the specific conversion that's failing."""
    print("Testing xyxy to rel_xyxy conversion with ragged images...")
    
    # Create ragged images with different sizes
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),  # First image: 100x100
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])     # Second image: 50x50
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    print(f"Ragged images shape: {ragged_images.shape}")
    print(f"Row lengths: {ragged_images.row_lengths()}")
    
    # Create bounding boxes in xyxy format
    # Format: [batch, num_boxes, 4] where 4 = [left, top, right, bottom]
    xyxy_boxes = tf.constant([
        [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0]],  # Box for first image (100x100)
        [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]    # Box for second image (50x50) 
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"XYXY boxes shape: {xyxy_boxes.shape}")
    print(f"XYXY boxes: {xyxy_boxes}")
    
    try:
        # Try to convert xyxy to rel_xyxy using ragged images
        result = bounding_box.convert_format(
            xyxy_boxes,
            source="xyxy",
            target="rel_xyxy",
            images=ragged_images
        )
        print(f"Conversion successful!")
        print(f"Result shape: {result.shape}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
        
        # Expected relative coordinates:
        # First image (100x100): [10/100, 20/100, 90/100, 80/100] = [0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]
        # Second image (50x50): [5/50, 10/50, 45/50, 40/50] = [0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]
        expected = tf.constant([
            [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]],
            [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]]
        ], dtype=tf.float32)
        print(f"Expected: {expected}")
        
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error occurred: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

Let me create a more detailed test to isolate the issue:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/keras-team__keras-cv__0.4/debug_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Debug script to understand the broadcasting issue in _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy with ragged images.
"""

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf

def debug_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy_step_by_step():
    """Debug the conversion step by step."""
    print("DEBUGGING _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy STEP BY STEP")
    print("=" * 50)
    
    # Import the internal function
    from keras_cv.bounding_box.converters import _image_shape
    
    # Create ragged images
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Create boxes
    xyxy_boxes = tf.constant([
        [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0]],  # Shape: (2, 1, 4)
        [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Input boxes shape: {xyxy_boxes.shape}")
    print(f"Input boxes: {xyxy_boxes}")
    print()
    
    # Get image dimensions using the internal function
    height, width = _image_shape(ragged_images, None, xyxy_boxes)
    print(f"Image height shape: {height.shape}, values: {height}")
    print(f"Image width shape: {width.shape}, values: {width}")
    print()
    
    # Simulate what happens in _xyxy_to_rel_xyxy
    ALL_AXES = [1, 1, 1, 1]
    left, top, right, bottom = tf.split(xyxy_boxes, ALL_AXES, axis=-1)
    print(f"After split - left shape: {left.shape}")
    print(f"After split - top shape: {top.shape}")
    print(f"After split - right shape: {right.shape}") 
    print(f"After split - bottom shape: {bottom.shape}")
    print()
    
    # The problematic division operations
    print("Performing division operations...")
    print(f"left shape: {left.shape}, width shape: {width.shape}")
    print(f"right shape: {right.shape}, width shape: {width.shape}")
    print(f"top shape: {top.shape}, height shape: {height.shape}")
    print(f"bottom shape: {bottom.shape}, height shape: {height.shape}")
    print()
    
    try:
        left_div = left / width
        right_div = right / width
        top_div = top / height
        bottom_div = bottom / height
        
        print(f"left / width result shape: {left_div.shape}")
        print(f"right / width result shape: {right_div.shape}")
        print(f"top / height result shape: {top_div.shape}")
        print(f"bottom / height result shape: {bottom_div.shape}")
        print()
        
        # Concatenate results
        result = tf.concat([left_div, top_div, right_div, bottom_div], axis=-1)
        print(f"Final concatenated result shape: {result.shape}")
        print(f"Final result: {result}")
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Error during division: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

</tool_response><|assistant|></think>Now I can see the issue! 

In lines 514-516, when both `images` and `boxes` are `RaggedTensor`, the code adds an extra dimension to `height` and `width`:
```python
if isinstance(boxes, tf.RaggedTensor):
    height = tf.expand_dims(height, axis=-1)
    width = tf.expand_dims(width, axis=-1)
```

But when `boxes` is a regular tensor (not ragged), this extra dimension is NOT added, so:
- `height` and `width` have shape `(batch_size, 1)` 
- `boxes` coordinates have shape `(batch_size, num_boxes, 1)` after splitting
- When dividing `(batch_size, num_boxes, 1)` by `(batch_size, 1)`, TensorFlow broadcasts incorrectly

The fix should ensure consistent shapes regardless of whether boxes are ragged or not.

## Phase 5. FIX ANALYSIS<tool_call>think<arg_key>thought</arg_key><arg_value>Let me clearly analyze the problem:

**Problem Statement:**
The `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` function breaks when operating on ragged images with regular (non-ragged) bounding boxes due to incorrect broadcasting during division operations.

**Where the problem is located:**
In `/workspace/keras-team__keras-cv__0.4/keras_cv/bounding_box/converters.py`, specifically in the `_image_shape` function (lines 501-519) and how it interacts with the `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` function (lines 223-235).

**How the test reproduces the issue:**
My debug script shows that when we have:
- Regular boxes: shape `(2, 1, 4)` - 2 batches, 1 box each
- Ragged images that produce height/width: shape `(2, 1)` - 2 batches  

During the division operations in `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy`:
- `left` has shape `(2, 1, 1)` 
- `width` has shape `(2, 1)`
- Broadcasting `left / width` incorrectly produces shape `(2, 2, 1)` instead of `(2, 1, 1)`

**Root Cause:**
In the `_image_shape` function, lines 514-516 add an extra dimension to height and width ONLY when boxes are RaggedTensor:
```python
if isinstance(boxes, tf.RaggedTensor):
    height = tf.expand_dims(height, axis=-1)
    width = tf.expand_dims(width, axis=-1)
```

But when boxes are regular tensors, no extra dimension is added, causing inconsistent broadcasting behavior.

**Best practices for the fix:**
1. Ensure consistent tensor shapes regardless of whether boxes are ragged or not
2. The height and width should have compatible shapes for broadcasting with box coordinates
3. Minimal change to avoid breaking existing functionality
4. Handle both ragged and dense scenarios properly

**How to fix the problem:**
The fix should ensure that height and width always have the correct shape for broadcasting with box coordinates. Looking at the working case with ragged boxes, the solution is to always add the extra dimension to height and width when dealing with ragged images, regardless of whether boxes are ragged or not.

Actually, let me reconsider this. Looking more carefully at the logic:

When images are ragged:
- Line 510: `height = tf.reshape(images.row_lengths(), (-1, 1))` → shape `(batch_size, 1)`
- Line 511-513: `width = tf.reshape(tf.reduce_max(images.row_lengths(axis=2), 1), (-1, 1))` → shape `(batch_size, 1)`
- Lines 514-516: ONLY if boxes are ragged, add another dimension

But for regular boxes, we want the height/width to broadcast properly with `(batch_size, num_boxes, 4)` box tensors. The box coordinates after splitting become `(batch_size, num_boxes, 1)`, so we need height/width to be `(batch_size, 1, 1)` to broadcast correctly.

So the fix is: when images are ragged, we should ALWAYS reshape height and width to `(batch_size, 1, 1)` regardless of box type.</arg_value></tool_call><|observation|><tool_response>Your thought has been logged.</tool_response><|assistant|></think>### 5.1 Problem Statement:
The `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy` function fails when operating on ragged images with regular (non-ragged) bounding boxes due to incorrect tensor broadcasting during division operations.

### 5.2 Problem Location:
The issue is in `/workspace/keras-team__keras-cv__0.4/keras_cv/bounding_box/converters.py` in the `_image_shape` function (lines 501-519), specifically lines 514-516.

### 5.3 How the Test Reproduces the Issue:
My debug script shows incorrect broadcasting:
- Input boxes: `(2, 1, 4)` - 2 batches, 1 box each  
- Image height/width from ragged images: `(2, 1)` - 2 batches
- After coordinate splitting: box coordinates become `(2, 1, 1)`
- Division `coordinate / dimension` broadcasts `(2, 1, 1)` with `(2, 1)` incorrectly to `(2, 2, 1)`

### 5.4 Best Practices for the Fix:
1. Ensure consistent tensor shapes for proper broadcasting
2. Make minimal changes to avoid breaking existing functionality  
3. Handle both ragged and dense scenarios uniformly
4. Preserve backward compatibility

### 5.5 How to Fix the Problem:
The root cause is in lines 514-516 where extra dimensions are added to height/width **only** when boxes are RaggedTensor. For proper broadcasting with box coordinates `(batch_size, num_boxes, 1)`, the image dimensions should always be shaped `(batch_size, 1, 1)` when dealing with ragged images.

The fix is to always add the extra dimension when images are ragged, regardless of box type.

The issue mentioned is with `random_shear_demo.py`, so let me trace through what that does:<tool_call>str_replace_editor<arg_key>path</arg_key><arg_value>/workspace/keras-team__keras-cv__0.4/test_real_issue.py</arg_value><arg_key>command</arg_key><arg_value>create</arg_value><arg_key>file_text</arg_key><arg_value>#!/usr/bin/env python3
"""
Test to reproduce the actual issue that would occur in random_shear_demo.py
"""

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf
from keras_cv.layers import preprocessing

def test_random_shear_with_ragged():
    """Test the scenario that would fail in the demo."""
    print("Testing RandomShear with ragged batch...")
    
    # Create sample data similar to what VOC would provide
    # But let's make it simpler and avoid downloading
    images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.random.uniform(0, 255, size=[100, 100, 3]).astype(np.float32),
        np.random.uniform(0, 255, size=[50, 50, 3]).astype(np.float32)
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Create bounding boxes in xyxy format
    bounding_boxes = {
        "boxes": tf.ragged.constant([
            [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0], [15.0, 25.0, 85.0, 75.0]],  # 2 boxes for first image
            [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]                               # 1 box for second image
        ], ragged_rank=1),
        "classes": tf.ragged.constant([
            [0.0, 1.0],  # 2 classes for first image
            [2.0]        # 1 class for second image
        ], ragged_rank=1)
    }
    
    print(f"Images shape: {images.shape}")
    print(f"Boxes structure: {bounding_boxes['boxes'].shape}")
    
    # Create the RandomShear layer like in the demo
    random_shear = preprocessing.RandomShear(
        x_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
        y_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
        bounding_box_format="xyxy",
    )
    
    try:
        # This should trigger the conversion internally
        result = random_shear({'images': images, 'bounding_boxes': bounding_boxes})
        print("RandomShear with ragged data: SUCCESS")
        print(f"Result images type: {type(result['images'])}")
        print(f"Result boxes type: {type(result['bounding_boxes']['boxes'])}")
        return True
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"RandomShear with ragged data: FAILED - {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

def test_conversion_that_fails():
    """Try to isolate the exact conversion that fails."""
    print("\nTesting specific conversions that might fail...")
    
    from keras_cv import bounding_box
    
    # Create ragged images
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Test various box formats that might be involved in RandomShear processing
    test_cases = [
        ("xyxy", "rel_xyxy"),
        ("xyxy", "xywh"), 
        ("xywh", "rel_xyxy"),
        ("rel_xyxy", "xyxy"),
    ]
    
    for source, target in test_cases:
        print(f"\n  Testing {source} -> {target}")
        try:
            # Use regular boxes first
            if source == "xyxy":
                boxes = tf.constant([
                    [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0]],
                    [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]
                ], dtype=tf.float32)
            elif source == "xywh":
                boxes = tf.constant([
                    [[10.0, 20.0, 80.0, 60.0]],
                    [[5.0, 10.0, 40.0, 30.0]]
                ], dtype=tf.float32)
            elif source == "rel_xyxy":
                boxes = tf.constant([
                    [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]],
                    [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]]
                ], dtype=tf.float32)
            
            result = bounding_box.convert_format(
                boxes, source=source, target=target, images=ragged_images
            )
            print(f"    SUCCESS - Result shape: {result.shape}")
            
        except Exception as e:
            print(f"    FAILED - {type(e).__name__}: {e}")

"""
Try to find the actual error that was occurring.

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf
from keras_cv import bounding_box

def test_problematic_scenario():
    """Try to find a scenario that actually breaks."""
    print("Testing scenarios that might break...")
    
    # Create ragged images
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    print(f"Ragged images shape: {ragged_images.shape}")
    
    # Test various combinations that might break
    test_cases = [
        # Different box shapes and formats
        {"boxes": tf.constant([[[10., 20., 90., 80.]]]), "format": "xyxy"},
        {"boxes": tf.constant([[[10., 20., 90., 80.]], [[5., 10., 45., 40.]]]), "format": "xyxy"},
        {"boxes": tf.ragged.constant([[[10., 20., 90., 80.]], [[5., 10., 45., 40.]]], ragged_rank=1), "format": "xyxy"},
    ]
    
    conversions = [
        ("xyxy", "rel_xyxy"),
        ("rel_xyxy", "xyxy"), 
        ("xyxy", "xywh"),
        ("xywh", "rel_xyxy")
    ]
    
    for i, case in enumerate(test_cases):
        boxes = case["boxes"]
        box_format = case["format"]
        print(f"\nTest case {i+1}: boxes shape {boxes.shape}, type {type(boxes)}")
        
        for source, target in conversions:
            try:
                if source == box_format:
                    result = bounding_box.convert_format(
                        boxes, source=source, target=target, images=ragged_images
                    )
                    print(f"  {source} -> {target}: SUCCESS, shape {result.shape}")
                else:
                    print(f"  {source} -> {target}: SKIPPED (wrong source format)")
            except Exception as e:
                print(f"  {source} -> {target}: FAILED - {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
                import traceback
                traceback.print_exc()

def test_edge_cases():
    """Test edge cases that might cause errors."""
    print("\n" + "="*50)
    print("TESTING EDGE CASES")
    print("="*50)
    
    # Very different image sizes
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[1000, 50, 3]),  # Wide image
        np.ones(shape=[50, 1000, 3])   # Tall image
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Boxes that might cause division issues
    boxes = tf.constant([
        [[0., 0., 1000., 50.]],    # Full size box for first image
        [[0., 0., 50., 1000.]]     # Full size box for second image
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Edge case boxes shape: {boxes.shape}")
    
    try:
        result = bounding_box.convert_format(
            boxes, source="xyxy", target="rel_xyxy", images=ragged_images
        )
        print(f"Edge case conversion SUCCESS: shape {result.shape}")
        print(f"Result: {result}")
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"Edge case conversion FAILED: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()

- Test case 2: `(2, 1, 4)` boxes → result shape `(2, 1, 4)` ✅ Correct!
- Edge case: result contains correct values

   508	            height, width = image_shape[1], image_shape[2]
   509	        else:
   510	            height = tf.reshape(images.row_lengths(), (-1, 1))
   511	            width = tf.reshape(
   512	                tf.reduce_max(images.row_lengths(axis=2), 1), (-1, 1)
   513	            )
   514	            # Always add extra dimension for proper broadcasting with box coordinates
   515	            height = tf.expand_dims(height, axis=-1)
   516	            width = tf.expand_dims(width, axis=-1)
   517	    else:
   518	        height, width = image_shape[0], image_shape[1]
   519	    return tf.cast(height, boxes.dtype), tf.cast(width, boxes.dtype)
   520

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf
from keras_cv import bounding_box

def test_correctness():
    """Test that the mathematical results are now correct."""
    print("Testing mathematical correctness of the fix...")
    
    # Create ragged images with known dimensions
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),  # 100x100 image
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])     # 50x50 image
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Test case: boxes at known positions
    xyxy_boxes = tf.constant([
        [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0]],  # Box in first image (100x100)
        [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]    # Box in second image (50x50)
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Input boxes: {xyxy_boxes}")
    print("Expected relative coordinates:")
    print(f"  First image (100x100): [10/100, 20/100, 90/100, 80/100] = [0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]")
    print(f"  Second image (50x50): [5/50, 10/50, 45/50, 40/50] = [0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]")
    
    # Convert to relative coordinates
    rel_result = bounding_box.convert_format(
        xyxy_boxes, source="xyxy", target="rel_xyxy", images=ragged_images
    )
    
    print(f"Actual result: {rel_result}")
    
    # Check if results are correct
    expected = tf.constant([
        [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]],
        [[0.1, 0.2, 0.9, 0.8]]
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Expected result: {expected}")
    
    # Check shape
    if rel_result.shape == expected.shape:
        print("✅ Shape is correct")
    else:
        print(f"❌ Shape is wrong: got {rel_result.shape}, expected {expected.shape}")
        return False
    
    # Check values (with small tolerance for floating point)
    if tf.reduce_all(tf.abs(rel_result - expected) < 1e-6):
        print("✅ Values are correct")
        return True
    else:
        print("❌ Values are wrong")
        print(f"Difference: {tf.abs(rel_result - expected)}")
        return False

def test_multiple_boxes():

        [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0], [2.0, 5.0, 48.0, 45.0]]     # 2 boxes for second image
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Input boxes shape: {xyxy_boxes.shape}")
    
    rel_result = bounding_box.convert_format(
        xyxy_boxes, source="xyxy", target="rel_xyxy", images=ragged_images
    )
    
    print(f"Result shape: {rel_result.shape}")
    print(f"Result: {rel_result}")
    
    # Expected calculations:
    # First image (100x200):
    #   Box 1: [10/200, 20/100, 90/200, 80/100] = [0.05, 0.2, 0.45, 0.8]
    #   Box 2: [5/200, 10/100, 95/200, 90/100] = [0.025, 0.1, 0.475, 0.9]
    # Second image (50x100):
    #   Box 1: [5/100, 10/50, 45/100, 40/50] = [0.05, 0.2, 0.45, 0.8]
    #   Box 2: [2/100, 5/50, 48/100, 45/50] = [0.02, 0.1, 0.48, 0.9]
    
    expected = tf.constant([
        [[0.05, 0.2, 0.45, 0.8], [0.025, 0.1, 0.475, 0.9]],
        [[0.05, 0.2, 0.45, 0.8], [0.02, 0.1, 0.48, 0.9]]
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    print(f"Expected: {expected}")
    
    if rel_result.shape == expected.shape:
        print("✅ Shape is correct")
    else:
        print(f"❌ Shape is wrong: got {rel_result.shape}, expected {expected.shape}")
        return False
        
    if tf.reduce_all(tf.abs(rel_result - expected) < 1e-6):
        print("✅ Values are correct")
        return True
    else:
        print("❌ Values are wrong")
        print(f"Difference: {tf.abs(rel_result - expected)}")
        return False

def test_roundtrip():
    """Test that converting to rel and back gives the original result."""
    print("\nTesting round-trip conversion...")
    
    ragged_images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.ones(shape=[100, 100, 3]),
        np.ones(shape=[50, 50, 3])
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    original_boxes = tf.constant([
        [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0]],
        [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]
    ], dtype=tf.float32)
    
    # Convert to relative
    rel_boxes = bounding_box.convert_format(
        original_boxes, source="xyxy", target="rel_xyxy", images=ragged_images
    )
    
    # Convert back to absolute
    abs_boxes = bounding_box.convert_format(
        rel_boxes, source="rel_xyxy", target="xyxy", images=ragged_images
    )
    
    print(f"Original: {original_boxes}")
    print(f"Round-trip: {abs_boxes}")
    
    if tf.reduce_all(tf.abs(abs_boxes - original_boxes) < 1e-6):
        print("✅ Round-trip conversion is accurate")
        return True
    else:
        print("❌ Round-trip conversion has errors")
        print(f"Difference: {tf.abs(abs_boxes - original_boxes)}")
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":

## Phase 8. FINAL REVIEW

Let me make sure I understand what I've accomplished and run some final verification:

### Summary of Changes Made

I identified and fixed a broadcasting issue in the `_image_shape` function in `/workspace/keras-team__keras-cv__0.4/keras_cv/bounding_box/converters.py`.

**The Problem:** 
When converting bounding box coordinates with ragged images, the `_image_shape` function was inconsistently adding dimensions to height/width tensors, causing incorrect broadcasting during division operations in functions like `_xyxy_to_rel_xyxy`. This led to mathematically incorrect results where:
- Input shape: `(2, 1, 4)` 
- Output shape: `(2, 2, 4)` instead of correct `(2, 1, 4)`

**The Root Cause:** 
Lines 514-516 in `_image_shape` only added extra dimensions when `boxes` was a `RaggedTensor`, but not for regular tensors, causing inconsistent broadcasting behavior.

**The Fix:**
Changed lines 514-516 to always add the extra dimension when dealing with ragged images, ensuring consistent tensor shapes for proper broadcasting:

```python
# OLD (buggy):
if isinstance(boxes, tf.RaggedTensor):
    height = tf.expand_dims(height, axis=-1)
    width = tf.expand_dims(width, axis=-1)

# NEW (fixed):
# Always add extra dimension for proper broadcasting with box coordinates
height = tf.expand_dims(height, axis=-1)
width = tf.expand_dims(width, axis=-1)
```

"""
Final test demonstrating that the RandomShear demo scenario works correctly.
"""

import numpy as np
import tensorflow as tf
from keras_cv.layers import preprocessing

def test_random_shear_scenario():
    """Test the exact scenario that would be in random_shear_demo.py"""
    print("Testing RandomShear scenario that was previously broken...")
    
    # Create ragged batch similar to what VOC dataset would produce
    images = tf.ragged.constant([
        np.random.uniform(0, 255, size=[100, 100, 3]).astype(np.float32),  # First image
        np.random.uniform(0, 255, size=[50, 50, 3]).astype(np.float32)     # Second image  
    ], ragged_rank=2)
    
    # Create bounding boxes in xyxy format (what VOC provides)
    bounding_boxes = {
        "boxes": tf.ragged.constant([
            [[10.0, 20.0, 90.0, 80.0], [15.0, 25.0, 85.0, 75.0]],  # 2 boxes for first image
            [[5.0, 10.0, 45.0, 40.0]]                               # 1 box for second image
        ], ragged_rank=1),
        "classes": tf.ragged.constant([
            [0.0, 1.0],  # Classes for first image boxes
            [2.0]        # Class for second image box
        ], ragged_rank=1)
    }
    
    print(f"Input images shape: {images.shape}")
    print(f"Input boxes structure: {bounding_boxes['boxes'].shape}")
    
    # Create RandomShear layer exactly like in the demo
    random_shear = preprocessing.RandomShear(
        x_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
        y_factor=(0.1, 0.5),
        bounding_box_format="xyxy",
    )
    
    try:
        # This operation would previously fail or produce wrong results
        result = random_shear({'images': images, 'bounding_boxes': bounding_boxes})
        
        print("✅ RandomShear with ragged images: SUCCESS")
        print(f"Output images type: {type(result['images'])}")
        print(f"Output boxes type: {type(result['bounding_boxes']['boxes'])}")
        
        # Verify the bounding box transformations are mathematically sound
        if hasattr(result['bounding_boxes']['boxes'], 'shape'):
            print(f"Output boxes shape: {result['bounding_boxes']['boxes'].shape}")
        
        return True
        
    except Exception as e:
        print(f"❌ RandomShear with ragged images: FAILED")
        print(f"Error: {type(e).__name__}: {e}")
        import traceback
        traceback.print_exc()
        return False

if __name__ == "__main__":
    print("=" * 60)
    print("FINAL DEMONSTRATION: RandomShear Demo Fix")
    print("=" * 60)
    
    success = test_random_shear_scenario()
    
    print("\n" + "=" * 60)
